To this end, we introduce Research Attention Prediction (RAP), a rolling, outcome-grounded distribution-forecasting benchmark for this question (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 1). ## 2 Related Work

Outcome-grounded scientific forecasting. Scientific f<sub>orecas</sub>ti<sub>ng spans aggrega</sub>t<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ren</sub>d<sub>s an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>v</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>ar</sub>t<sub>e-</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> E<sub>ar</sub>li<sub>er sc</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t<sub>ome</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c wor</sub>k <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> t<sub>op</sub>i<sub>c evo</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d emer<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> areas (Grifiths and Ste<sub>y</sub>vers 2004; Blei and Laffert<sub>y</sub> 2006; Chen 2006; Small 2006), forecasts field or subfield activity and topic prevalence (Taşkın 2021; Asooja et al. 2016; Ofer, Kaufman, and Linial 2024), and <sub>p</sub>redicts future links or hi<sub>g</sub>h-im<sub>p</sub>act conce<sub>p</sub>ts in scientific <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs (Krenn et al. 2023; Gu and Krenn 2025; Marwitz et al. 2026). Re-<sub>cen</sub>t b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s</sub> f<sub>orecas</sub>t <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l <sub>resu</sub>lt<sub>s or sc</sub>i<sub>en</sub>tifi<sub>c</sub> events (Luo et al. 2025; Wu et al. 2026), future-<sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er com<sub>p</sub>onents and im<sub>p</sub>act (Ajith et al. 2026), researchjud<sub>g</sub>ments (Tian et al. 2026), or the future ali<sub>g</sub>nment of ideas and <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osals (Jian<sub>g</sub> 2026; Wan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2026). RAP instead <sub>p</sub>redicts a rolling, jointly normalised field-level distribution over a f<sub>rozen co</sub>d<sub>e</sub>b<sub>oo</sub>k <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>ti<sub>ve ev</sub>id<sub>ence acqu</sub>i<sub>s</sub>iti<sub>on, an</sub>d <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>rs</sub> F<sub>orecas</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub> <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d St<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erven</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>-con</sub>diti<sub>one</sub>d <sub>searc</sub>h f<sub>rom</sub> t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>rea</sub>d<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Temporal evaluation and forecasting. Temporal evalu-<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on mo</sub>ti<sub>va</sub>t<sub>es</sub> ti<sub>me-sens</sub>iti<sub>ve an</sub>d d<sub>ynam</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d tests (Lazaridou et al. 2021; Li, Guerin, and Lin 2024; Kar<sub>g</sub>er et al. 2025); re<sub>p</sub>orted cut-ofs ma<sub>y</sub> difer from efective ones, and <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ted simulated i<sub>g</sub>norance is unreliable (Chen<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024; Li et al. 2026). RAP follows rollin<sub>g</sub>-ori<sub>g</sub>in <sub>p</sub>ractice, <sub>compares aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t <sub>pers</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ence, exponen</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l<sub>-smoo</sub>thi<sub>ng, an</sub>d no-chan<sub>g</sub>e references (H<sub>y</sub>ndman and Athanaso<sub>p</sub>oulos 2021; Beck, Dovern, and Vo<sub>g</sub>l 2025), and treats its tar<sub>g</sub>et as a com-<sub>p</sub>ositional share vector (Sn<sub>y</sub>der et al. 2017).

Time-invariant assignment and rolling targets. For field f, let $\mathcal { D } _ { f } = \{ d _ { 1 } , \ldots , d _ { 8 } \}$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> it<sub>s</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> A d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e-</sub> bli<sub>n</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>ifi<sub>er uses eac</sub>h <sub>paper</sub>’<sub>s</sub> titl<sub>e an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t t<sub>oge</sub>th<sub>er</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> <sub>wr</sub>itt<sub>en</sub> <sub>co</sub>d<sub>e</sub>b<sub>oo</sub>k t<sub>o</sub> <sub>ass</sub>i<sub>gn</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>mary</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l i<sub>n</sub> $\mathcal { D } _ { f }$ or <sub>other.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> <sub>ru</sub>l<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>use</sub>d <sub>a</sub>t <sub>every</sub> <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>n.</sub>

For forecast origin T and direction $d \in \mathcal { D } _ { f }$ <sub>,</sub> l<sub>e</sub>t $n _ { f , T , d }$ b<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>papers</sub> h<sub>o</sub>ldi<sub>ng</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>mem</sub>b<sub>ers</sub>hi<sub>p</sub> i<sub>n</sub> fi<sub>e</sub>ld $f ,$ <sub>,</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t submitted on or after T and before $T + 6$ <sub>mon</sub>th<sub>s,</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>rece</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> primary label d. The realised target share is

$$
y _ { f , T , d } = \frac { n _ { f , T , d } } { \sum _ { d ^ { \prime } \in \mathcal { D } _ { f } } n _ { f , T , d ^ { \prime } } } ,
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $d ^ { \prime }$ i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>exes</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>e</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> Th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore,</sub> $y _ { f , T } =$ $( y _ { f , T , d } ) _ { d \in \mathcal { D } _ { f } }$ i<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub> <sub>e</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>-</sub>di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>non-nega</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>compo-</sub> sition summin<sub>g</sub> to one (Sn<sub>y</sub>der et al. 2017). ## D.2 Compositional-distance sensitivity

Th<sub>e percen</sub>t<sub>age</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>ace</sub> f<sub>orces an agen</sub>t t<sub>o express re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>d<sub>eo</sub>f<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so</sub> <sub>exposes</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>are</sub> <sub>magn</sub>it<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>secon</sub>d<sub>ary</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l<sub>-var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance</sub>

$$
\mathrm { T V } ( p , y ) = { \frac { 1 } { 2 } } \sum _ { d = 1 } ^ { 8 } \left| p _ { d } - y _ { d } \right|
$$

where d indexes the eight frozen directions. We also com-<sub>p</sub>ute Jensen–Shannon diver<sub>g</sub>ence (natural lo<sub>g</sub>arithms, without smoothin<sub>g</sub>) on the same normalized vectors. Lower val-<sub>ues are</sub> b<sub>e</sub>tt<sub>er.</sub> P<sub>a</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d “i<sub>mprovemen</sub>t<sub>s</sub>” b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow are</sub> th<sub>e re</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance m</sub>i<sub>nus</sub> th<sub>e can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance, so pos</sub>iti<sub>ve va</sub>l<sub>ues</sub> f<sub>a-</sub> <sub>vor</sub> th<sub>e can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e.</sub>

F<sub>or norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>compos</sub>iti<sub>ons</sub> $a , b ,$ d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> th<sub>e</sub> K<sub>u</sub>llb<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>–</sub> L<sub>e</sub>ibl<sub>er</sub> di<sub>vergence</sub> <sub>as</sub> $\begin{array} { r } { \dot { \mathrm { K L } } ( { a } \| { b } ) = \sum _ { d : a _ { d } > 0 } a _ { d } \log ( a _ { d } / b _ { d } ) } \end{array}$ W<sub>r</sub>iti<sub>ng</sub> $m = ( p + y ) / 2$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>e repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d J<sub>ensen–</sub>Sh<sub>annon</sub> di<sub>-</sub> ver<sub>g</sub>ence <sup>i</sup>s

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathrm { J S D } ( p , y ) = \frac 1 2 \mathrm { K L } ( p \| m ) + \frac 1 2 \mathrm { K L } ( y \| m ) . } \end{array}
$$
